# PeakXR gameplay mechanics coverage inventory

Checkable list of every mechanic a VR player can meet in PEAK 2.6.b, one table row each, so each can be designed, implemented and tested in VR. Read `docs/design.md` first for the feature ids used in the Feature column. The source of truth is the decompile in `decomp/` (Unity 6000.3, Mono); prefab, scene and Animator data are not in it, so anything that depends on them is marked UNVERIFIED and listed in section (c). Research already written is cited as R1 (`docs/research/game-player-items-net.md`), R2 (`docs/research/game-ui-settings-render.md`) and R3 (`docs/research/creative-test-mode.md`) instead of repeated.

Nothing here has been run in a headset. Every file:line was checked by script against the decompile (file exists, line in range, named member near the line) and spot-read, but behaviour that needs the game running is in section (c).

## How to read the tables

- Columns: ID, Mechanic, Classes (`file:line`, relative to `decomp/Assembly-CSharp/`; `../` means another assembly under `decomp/`), Vanilla input / trigger, Camera / UI involvement, Proposed VR interaction (physical), Button fallback, Feature, VR risk, Test steps, Hands, Custom VR anim.
- ID prefix: IC and IT items, IN interactables, CH character and input, ST statuses and screen effects, EN mobs and hazards, MF meta flows. Several classes appear in more than one row when different mechanics share code; Appendix C lists those clusters.
- Feature: an id from `docs/design.md`, or `new:<name>` when none fits. `(partial)` means the existing feature covers part of the row. The new names are defined in section (a).
- VR risk starts with a tag: SICKNESS (camera or body motion the player did not make), UI (screen-space or overlay UI), PRECISION (aim, reach or gaze tolerances), NET (what other players see), NONE.
- Hands and Custom VR anim apply to item rows and read `n/a` elsewhere. Rules: the hands are independent, a player holds one item at a time, and an item is OneHanded, TwoHanded or OptionalTwoHand. `Hands` gives the class and the reason (size, aiming need, vanilla two-hand animation, grip points). `Custom VR anim` is `none` when a vanilla hold or use animation fits a physical grip, else `yes: <what is missing>`.
- Hands classes are design proposals. Grip transforms (`Hand_R`, `Hand_L`), `Item.rightHandOnly` and `Item.defaultPos` live in prefabs, so only the code facts quoted in the rows are verified.
- Test steps assume the `sandbox` feature (R3) for spawning, or name the scene and the trigger.

## Part A. Items

### Item handling core (42 rows)

Column legend for the two item-class columns: `Hands` = OneHanded / TwoHanded / OptionalTwoHand plus reason; `Custom VR anim` = `none` or `yes: <gap>`. Code facts that apply to every item row: `CharacterItems.GetItemPosRightWorld/LeftWorld` do `item.transform.Find("Hand_R")` / `Find("Hand_L")` with no null check (CharacterItems.cs:859-867), so every item prefab has both grip children; the left hand is welded and IK-weighted only when `Item.rightHandOnly` is false (CharacterItems.cs:648; CharacterAnimations.cs:353). `rightHandOnly`, grip transforms and `defaultPos` are prefab data, so per-item hand class is UNVERIFIED unless stated. Hold pose is head-relative: `animationItemTransform` plus `Item.defaultPos` (Item.cs:109). Feature ids are from design.md or the shared `new:` list.

#### Sticky items, thorns, fake items

### Climbing, rope and flight tools (17 rows)

Conventions for this fragment. "Primary" = `CharacterInput.usePrimaryIsPressed` through `CharacterItems.DoUsing` (`CharacterItems.cs:197`): press starts, hold fills `castProgress` over `Item.usingTimePrimary`, `OnPrimaryFinishedCast` fires at the end, release cancels (R1 §5.1). "Secondary" is the same with `useSecondary`. "Scroll" = `scrollInput` / `scrollForward|BackwardIsPressed` routed to `Item.Scroll*` (`CharacterItems.cs:229-248`). `usingTimePrimary`, `ItemAction` trigger flags, `Hand_R`/`Hand_L` grip children, `rightHandOnly` and `defaultPos` are prefab data, so every Hands cell is a design judgement (UNVERIFIED against the prefab) except where a code line is cited. Every held item is posed head-relative by `CharacterRagdoll.cs:224` (`animationItemTransform = animationLookTransform.TransformPoint(item.defaultPos)`) and the hands are IK-welded to the item grips (`CharacterAnimations.cs:390`, `CharacterItems.cs:641`, `CharacterAnimations.cs:353` zeroes the left IK weight when `rightHandOnly`). For VR the avatar.local/interact.physical layer must invert that (item follows the controller); this applies to all item rows and is not repeated in the Custom VR anim column.

| IN-01 | Interaction target selection (one best IInteractible per frame) | Interaction.cs:5; Interaction.cs:78; Interaction.cs:196 | LateUpdate (order 600) when canInteract (Interaction.cs:59: not passedOut/fullyPassedOut, CanDoInput = no window/wheel, no held StickyItemComponent). Ray from MainCamera.instance.transform position/forward, 2 m (distance), LineCheckAll with triggers, nearest hit that is not own ragdoll or held item, GetComponentInParent IInteractible + IsInteractible. Fallback SphereCast radius 0.5 from +0.25 m, smallest angle to forward, needs clear TerrainMap line. maxCharacterInteractAngle 90 is only used by CharacterInteractible. See R1 §5.4 | Result is Interaction.currentHovered/bestInteractable/bestCharacter; read by GUIManager prompt, highlight, feeding (Item.cs:984). No camera write | Hand reach volume (sphere ~0.15 m at palm) picks nearest IInteractible, plus hand pointing ray up to 2 m for far/large objects (rope, fuse). Write result in a DoInteractableRaycasts postfix and set currentHovered so prompt/highlight stay in sync | Vanilla gaze ray from HMD (MainCamera is HMD camera under rig.camera) | interact.physical | PRECISION: gaze ray is HMD-centred so it works unmodified, but hand volume must exclude own body and held item; two hands compete for one target | Spawn item/luggage in front, look at it (gaze) then reach with each hand. Check prompt and outline follow the hand target | n/a | n/a |

Press frame always calls Interact(); if IInteractibleConstant.IsConstantlyInteractable then currentHeldInteractible = it, timer = GetInteractTime. Timer grows with Time.deltaTime only while it stays the best target; at time Interact_CastFinished fires, then (holdOnFinish false everywhere) CancelCast is called and timer resets. Release or target change calls CancelCast. readyToInteract needs a release between presses | Progress ring via Interaction.constantInteractableProgress (IN-04) | Grip squeeze while a hand overlaps the target = press; keep squeezing = hold; move hand out or release = cancel. Add 0.2 s grace so hand jitter does not reset the timer. ## Part E. Mobs, hazards and environment events

### Creatures and bosses (20 rows)

Shared facts for all rows: `Character.Fall(s)` (`Character.cs:1735`) ragdolls the body and the vanilla camera follows the head/torso rigids (R2 §7 #2, R1 §2). Scripted `lookValues` writes on the victim fight an HMD-driven yaw/pitch (R1 §2.2). `GamefeelHandler.AddPerlinShake*` is the screen shake (R2 §7 #3). Statuses are named only (Poison, Injury, Web, FlyTrap, Spores, Hot, Cold, Drowsy, Thorns); their screen effects belong to the ST fragment.

## Part F. Meta flows: lobby, run lifecycle, menus, settings

meta flows, menus and run lifecycle. Paths are relative to decomp/Assembly-CSharp/. A path starting with `../` is under decomp/ (Zorro.* assemblies). R1/R2 = research docs. Cross-references to other fragments use the category prefix only (IN, IC, IT, EN, ST, CH) because their row numbers are not known here.

Hand class counts over the item rows (IC and IT). Rows marked n/a are not held items (slots, cooking, feeding, UI, worn gear).

| Class | Rows | IDs |
|---|---|---|
| OptionalTwoHand | 20 | IC-01, IC-06, IC-21, IC-29, IC-37, IC-39, IC-54, IC-55, IC-62, IC-63, IT-03, IT-07, IT-11, IT-24, IT-27, IT-31, IT-33, IT-35, IT-42, IT-53 |
| n/a | 42 | not a held item: slots, cooking, feeding, UI (all IC non-item rows) and worn, placed or fixed gear in IT: IT-04, IT-05, IT-06, IT-10, IT-12, IT-13, IT-30, IT-34, IT-47, IT-55, IT-58 |
| TwoHanded | 5 | IC-25, IC-65, IT-15, IT-36, IT-43 |
| OneHanded | 57 | IC-26, IC-27, IC-28, IC-30, IC-41, IC-43, IC-44, IC-45, IC-46, IC-47, IC-48, IC-49, IC-50, IC-51, IC-52, IC-53, IC-56, IC-58, IC-59, IC-60, IC-61, IC-64, IC-67, IT-01, IT-02, IT-08, IT-09, IT-14, IT-16, IT-17, IT-18, IT-19, IT-20, IT-21, IT-22, IT-23, IT-25, IT-26, IT-28, IT-29, IT-32, IT-37, IT-38, IT-39, IT-40, IT-41, IT-44, IT-45, IT-46, IT-48, IT-49, IT-50, IT-51, IT-52, IT-54, IT-56, IT-57 |
| UNVERIFIED (not classified) | 1 | IC-38 |

Items that need a new hold or use animation in VR because no vanilla animation fits a one-hand or two-hand physical grip. Vanilla poses every held item head-relative (`CharacterRagdoll.cs:224`) and welds the hands to the item grips (`CharacterAnimations.cs:390`, `CharacterItems.cs:641`), which is the opposite of a tracked grip, so the hold pose itself needs inverting for all items. The list below is the extra work beyond that.

| ID | Item | Missing in vanilla |
|---|---|---|
| IC-01 | Pick up a ground item | pickup ends in vanilla hold pose (head-relative) not at the grabbing hand; needs per-hand grip pose |
| IC-06 | Equip: spawn held item and weld to hands | vanilla hold pose is head-relative, no per-hand pose, left IK weight only on/off (CharacterAnimations.cs:353) |
| IC-13 | Use animation, SFX and cast shake | bools Eat/Heal/Drink/Antidote drive body-relative animation; must be suppressed or replaced by hand-driven poses |
| IC-19 | Throw charge | vanilla throw animation (`throwTime`) is a fixed body animation, needs hand-driven wind-up |
| IC-21 | Ground backpack: open wheel, hover and stabilise | no vanilla open-pack animation to hands |
| IC-25 | Hold and drop the backpack slot | held-pack pose has no per-hand grip data |
| IC-28 | Thorn and arrow removal | no pluck animation for hand IK |
| IC-30 | Cook a held item at a lit campfire | no hand-held-over-fire pose; vanilla is body-fixed |
| IC-39 | Use item on a friend | no vanilla hand-extended feed pose for hand IK |
| IC-41 | Extra feed behaviours | stab/offer pose absent for hand IK |
| IC-43 | Eat food, berries, packaged food | vanilla Eat animation is body-fixed hold-in-front; hand IK must stay at the mouth |
| IC-44 | Drink | no tilt-to-mouth pose; vanilla Drink anim assumes head-relative hold |
| IC-45 | Heal (bandage, medkit class) | no apply-to-body pose |
| IC-46 | Antidote and poison cure | reuses IC-44 pose gap |
| IC-65 | Passport item | open-book hand pose |
| IT-09 | Climbing spike: hammer a piton into a wall (also while climbing) | hammering swing replaces hold-to-cast bar (no vanilla anim) |
| IT-12 | Rocketpack: worn rocket, lit by fuse, carries the wearer for 3 s | camera takeover and tumbling assume third-person view; needs VR body-lock or fade, no per-hand pose |
| IT-15 | Glider: held wing gives forward glide while airborne | item rotation is forced from lookDirection (CharacterRagdoll.cs:226); needs hand-driven wing pose |
| IT-24 | Binoculars: toggle zoom view, scroll adjusts FOV | item raise is a defaultPos animation toward the face and the view is replaced; needs eyepiece render-texture lens |
| IT-25 | Camera override action (generic) | any override view must go to a screen, not the HMD |
| IT-26 | Magic bugle: hold to toot, affects nearby players | item raise to mouth is a defaultPos animation (BugleSFX.cs:117); replace with mouth-proximity pose |
| IT-27 | Guidebook: open book in front of camera, flip pages | opened book is locked to camera forward (Guidebook.cs:180); needs hand-anchored open pose |
| IT-28 | Guidebook page scroll: consume to unlock a page | single page also camera-locked when open |
| IT-29 | Passport item opens the passport menu | opening UI must be a panel attached to the held item |
| IT-36 | Antizooka: charge shot, spawns anti-gravity bubble at aim point | charge shake and recoil are defaultPos animations (Action_Antizooka.cs:80,87) |
| IT-42 | Basketball: dribble animation while running, throw | scripted dribble moves the item via defaultPos (Basketball.cs:79) |
| IT-43 | Snowball: grows when rolled, heavy when large | grips LeftHand/RightHand move with growth; carry pose |

## Appendix B. Input action coverage and code findings that affect the design

Input actions and where they are covered:

- Move, MoveForward/Backward/Left/Right, Look: CH-02
- Jump: CH-13; Sprint/SprintToggle: CH-11; Crouch/CrouchToggle: CH-12
- Interact: CH-03; UsePrimary/UseSecondary: CH-04; Drop: CH-05
- Scroll, ScrollForward, ScrollBackward: CH-07
- Hotbar1-4, SelectSlotForward/Backward, UnselectSlot: CH-06
- Emote: CH-48; Ping: CH-52; Pause: CH-08; PushToTalk: CH-53

Findings from the sweep that differ from, or are missing in, R1 and R2:

- R1 §3.2 interact: held Interact while fully passed out speeds your own death timer 0.33 per s (Character.cs:1177-1181), it is not a hold-to-revive timer
- R1 §3.2 selectBackpackWasPressed has no consumer (CharacterInput.cs:257); Hotbar5-9 have no consumer; crouchWasPressed has no consumer
- R1 §4.2: under Gamepad scheme release of UsePrimary does not stop a climb (CharacterClimbing.cs:146, :572); climbs also refuse to start with carrierCondor set (CharacterClimbing.cs:562)
- R1 §4.3: no ladder class, but a ClimbModifierSurface with staminaUsageMultiplier <= 0.34 shows the ladder reticle (GUIManager.cs:706)
- R1 §2.1: PlayerGhost instantiate is at MainCameraMovement.cs:146 (not :143); FovSetting below 60 falls back to 70
- R2 §7 row 3: the AddPerlinShake at CharacterMovement.cs:927 is the steep-slope slide shake, plain landing has no shake (CharacterMovement.cs:817-830)
- OnlyOneEdgeModifier, FreeModifier, UniformModifier (named in the CH sweep) are UI corner-radius modifiers, not climb or movement code
- HideTheBody hides a body you are carrying from you (HideTheBody.cs:42); R1 §2.1 does not mention it
- ResetInput leaves PTT, ping, scroll, spectate and sprintToggleIsPressed unchanged while input is blocked (CharacterInput.cs:297 vs :212)

Other findings from the item and hazard sweeps:

- `ItemAction.OnReleased` is never subscribed, `Item.ContinueUseSecondary` tests `usingTimePrimary` rather than a secondary time, `Action_WarpRandomly` has no `RunAction`, `Action_PlayAnimation` is a no-op, and the master ignores the cooked amount that `Action_ConsumeAndSpawn` sends.
- No class named for a chain exists in the decompile; chains are not a separate mechanic. Ladders are rope or `ClimbModifierSurface` climbs.
- Chaos does not scramble camera or input (ST-34), Numb only swaps HUD canvas groups (ST-41), `PreventPoisonHealing` and `ClimbingChalk` have no reader (ST-25, ST-42).
- Photosensitive mode is cached by `GUIManager` at Start (`GUIManager.cs:353`) and hidden in the settings UI once in a room, so the mod has to set it before Start.
- The `lookValues` write at `MushroomZombie.cs:762` targets the zombie itself. Writes to a victim's look direction are in `FrogTongue.cs:430,453` and `Scoutmaster.cs:591`.
- `isGodCam` is never assigned in code, so the Bing Bong god-cam powers (IT-58) may be unreachable.
- R2 §1.6 puts the `CloseAllWindows` and win/loss logic in `Character.EndGame`; it is in `RPCEndGame` (`Character.cs:893`). R2 also omits the credits wait (`PeakHandler.cs:223`), `PlayerConnectionLog`, and that the `PassOut` and `FallDeath` transitions are never played. `CrossPlatformSetting` and `AudioModeSetting` are not registered in `SettingsHandler`, and `ReadyUpToTrigger`, `CosmeticUnlockWindow`, `GoToAirport` and `UI_Notifications.AddNotification` have no callers.
- Revive in vanilla comes from `RespawnChest`, the Book of Bones, `ScoutEffigy`, `RespawnRandomScout`, a `MapHandler` void rescue and rejoin. Holding Interact while passed out only speeds the death timer (`Character.cs:1177`).

- Gate portal: IN-34, EN-57, ST-69, MF-46. Pillar: IN-33, EN-20. Ghost ball: EN-17, IT-55.
- Carry: IN-13, CH-43, CH-44. Reach, help-up and kick: IN-16, IN-17, CH-28, CH-29.
- Rope, vine, handle: IN-38, CH-26, IT-04; IN-39, CH-27, IT-07; IN-37, CH-25, IT-10.
- Cannon: IN-35, IT-34. Rocketpack and fuse: IN-35, IT-12, CH-61.
- Backpack: IC-21 to IC-25, IN-09, IN-10, CH-51.
- Photosensitive and bug phobia settings: ST-70, ST-71, MF-85, MF-87. Colorblind: ST-73, MF-86.
- Fade and transitions: CH-34, CH-40, ST-54, MF-56, MF-102.

- Biome (Biome.cs:3), BiomeSelector (BiomeSelector.cs:6), BiomeVariant (BiomeVariant.cs:3) - scene variant selection and biome enum
- LevelGeneration (LevelGeneration.cs:5), MapGenerator (MapGenerator.cs:4) - seeded prop generation, editor and console triggered
- CinemaCamera (CinemaCamera.cs:3) - only turns on with Editor or debug build and C+M keys
- TestEndScreen (TestEndScreen.cs:9), EndScreenStatus (EndScreenStatus.cs:1) - debug test class, internal status flag
- CosmeticUnlockWindow (CosmeticUnlockWindow.cs:3) - MenuWindow subclass with no references; EndScreen draws its own unlock popups
- GoToAirport (GoToAirport.cs:4) - helper with no callers (end path is MF-55)
- PauseMainMenu (PauseMainMenu.cs:3), Peak.UI/ConfirmPage (Peak.UI/ConfirmPage.cs:9) - no callers found; confirm dialog used instead is MF-62
- Peak.UI/SelectableEvents, SelectablePassThrough, SelectableSlider (Peak.UI/*.cs) - selection plumbing for the player list slider (MF-20)
- UIWheel, UIWheelSlice, EmoteWheel, BackpackWheel (UIWheel.cs:5, EmoteWheel.cs:7, BackpackWheel.cs:7) - radial wheels belong to the emote/inventory fragments (new:radial-wheels); GUIManager hooks are GUIManager.cs:561 and GUIManager.cs:546
- Peak/ScoutsHonor (Peak/ScoutsHonor.cs:3) - amulet item (IT)
- Peak/IntegratedGraphicsHelper (Peak/IntegratedGraphicsHelper.cs:8) - GPU culling toggle at boot, no player-facing option
- AchievementCLIParser (AchievementCLIParser.cs:6), AchievementManagerDebug (AchievementManagerDebug.cs:5), ReconnectDataDebugPage (ReconnectDataDebugPage.cs:8), NetworkStats (NetworkStats.cs:8), NetworkStatsPage - dev tooling
- OnscreenKeyboard (../Zorro.Core.Runtime/OnscreenKeyboard.cs:5) - UI Toolkit debug keyboard, not used by the game UI
- AchievementManager (AchievementManager.cs:14) - ThrowAchievement calls PlatformManager.Platform.UnlockAchievement (L583-600); the game draws no toast (only the debug-build line in PlayerConnectionLog.cs:150); popup comes from the Steam overlay
- MountainProgressHandler area achievements (MountainProgressHandler.cs:220) - CheckAreaAchievement throws achievements silently; the visible part is the title card (MF-45)
- RichPresenceService (RichPresenceService.cs:6), SteamRichPresence (SteamRichPresence.cs:4) - status shown only in the Steam friends list, not in game
- CrossPlatformSetting (CrossPlatformSetting.cs:7), AudioModeSetting (AudioModeSetting.cs:5) - defined but not registered in SettingsHandler (SettingsHandler.cs:32-69), so not reachable in the menu; NetworkConnector.cs:346 would call GetSetting<CrossPlatformSetting>() on the cross-play path
- Zorro rumble/glyph helpers (../Zorro.ControllerSupport/...Rumble, ...Glyphs) - glyph and haptics plumbing already covered by xr.input (R2 §5)

## (a) Mechanics no current feature covers

Rows whose Feature column names a `new:` feature are not covered by anything in `docs/design.md`. Existing features cover the rest, fully or partly.

| Proposed feature | Rows | Scope | Row IDs |
|---|---|---|---|
| new:comfort-events | 101 | One policy for events that move or tint the view without player input: knockbacks, launches, ragdoll tumbles, forced `lookValues` writes, Perlin shake, status flashes, post volumes, teleports. Design.md only has rules ("no shake", "fade when the game takes the camera"). Needs a per-event table of fade, vignette or haptic substitutes. Could be folded into `rig.camera` plus `render.fixes`. Implemented by `comfort.events` (policy table in `PeakXR.Core/ComfortEvents.cs`). Teleports through `Character.WarpPlayer`, run shakes and the beach wake-up belong to `ui.runflow`. | IC-13, IC-33, IC-34, IC-35, IC-47, IC-49, IC-50, IC-52, IC-53, IC-54, IC-62, IC-63, IT-05, IT-12, IT-23, IT-34, IT-39, IT-45, IT-47, IT-52, IT-55, IN-18, IN-24, IN-25, IN-36, CH-16, CH-21, CH-30, CH-31, CH-32, CH-33, CH-34, CH-39, ST-07, ST-09, ST-10, ST-12, ST-13, ST-15, ST-16, ST-17, ST-19, ST-20, ST-23, ST-24, ST-26, ST-27, ST-29, ST-30, ST-31, ST-34, ST-38, ST-39, ST-40, ST-44, ST-52, ST-58, ST-70, EN-01, EN-02, EN-03, EN-04, EN-06, EN-07, EN-08, EN-09, EN-11, EN-13, EN-14, EN-15, EN-16, EN-17, EN-21, EN-23, EN-25, EN-27, EN-29, EN-30, EN-36, EN-39, EN-40, EN-42, EN-43, EN-44, EN-45, EN-46, EN-47, EN-48, EN-49, EN-52, EN-55, EN-56, MF-23, MF-38, MF-39, MF-44, MF-46, MF-51, MF-57, MF-59, MF-85 |

Two observations for the owner of `docs/design.md`. First, `new:comfort-events` is the largest gap by far: the design states comfort rules but no feature owns the list of game events (launches, grabs, scripted looks, shakes, status flashes, teleports) that break them. Second, every item row needs the hold pose inverted (item follows the controller, not the head), which `interact.physical` implies but does not spell out; the aimed-tool rows need the `new:item-aim` ray source swap at every call site that reads `MainCamera`.

## (b) The 15 highest-risk items for comfort or usability

| Rank | Rows | Risk | Direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | CH-31, CH-34, ST-53, MF-38 | Ragdoll tumble and the 5 s pass-out ramp roll the camera with head-bone physics, with motion blur on top. The forced 7 s ragdoll at every run start (MF-38) is the first thing a new player sees. | Never roll the HMD rig. Fade early, show a world-locked or frozen view, and keep the horizon level. |
| 2 | ST-63, IT-03, EN-49, EN-29, IN-33 | The Perlin shake adds up to half the amount in degrees of pitch and yaw to the camera rotation (`MainCameraMovement.cs:297`); curse is 30x, so about 15 degrees. Rope shooter (30), bridge (400) and tornado shake are the same system. | Zero the shake at the single sink and send the amplitude to controller haptics. |
| 3 | EN-04, EN-06 | Frog tongue and Scoutmaster grab write the victim's `lookValues`, drag the body and launch it (1500 force, 3.2 s forced look). | Ignore scripted look writes for the HMD, vignette hard, optional fade during the grab. |
| 4 | IT-12, CH-61 | Rocketpack forces a third-person camera plus torque steering for about 4 s. | Keep first-person pose, replace the third-person camera with a fade or a detached wide view. |
| 5 | IT-11 | Rescue hook fires from a screen-centre ray, forces the victim's look and launches both players. | Hand ray for aim, same policy as rank 3 for the victim. |
| 6 | IN-24, IN-25, CH-33, MF-44, MF-46, MF-57, MF-58, MF-59 | Revive, checkpoint, void rescue, segment jump and death move the HMD origin or cut to a ghost camera with no fade. | One fade-and-recenter helper for every teleport and camera swap. |
| 7 | IN-34, EN-57, ST-69, IT-23 | Portal and warp compass drive a warp volume with lens distortion -1, chromatic aberration and shake while the player holds the button. | Disable lens distortion and chromatic aberration in the volume; use a world-space effect instead. |
| 8 | ST-59, MF-85 | Screen VFX quads are sized from the mono `Camera.main` frustum (`CameraQuad.cs:11`), so they are wrong per eye, and they flash on every 0.025 status step. | Replace with per-eye or head-locked quads at fixed brightness; throttle flashes. |
| 9 | MF-49, MF-50 | The win cutscene disables `MainCamera` and drives scripted cameras with FOV 28 to 70. | Keep the rig alive, play the cutscene on a virtual screen or in world space with a fade. |
| 10 | EN-29, EN-09 | Tornado spin and orbit, and condor carry for tens of metres, are long passive motions the player cannot steer. | Heavy vignette, optional fade, haptic cues; consider a comfort option that skips the carry view. |
| 11 | CH-44, IN-13 | A carried player's camera follows the carrier running and jumping; the carrier's ragdoll is pulled onto `carryPosRef` with extra weight and drag. | Fade or lock the carried view to a slow seat; stabilise the carrier's head. |
| 12 | IC-06 | The vanilla hold pose is head-relative and `HoldItem` applies a look-driven force, so it fights tracked hands. Every item row depends on it. | Make the item follow the controller grip and disable the head-relative force for the local VR player. |
| 13 | IC-22, CH-48, CH-49 | The backpack and emote wheels are screen-space overlays driven by a pointer, and camera look is frozen while they are open. | World-space wheels around the hand, with pointer input from the laser or hand position. |
| 14 | IN-06, IN-11, IC-27, IC-28, IN-16 | Thorn, tick and stuck-item removal use 5 degree and 2 degree gaze cones and a 10 degree up/down gate; reach and help-up use an HMD cone and pull at force 70. | Replace the gaze rays with hand overlap tests. |
| 15 | MF-11, MF-21, MF-20 | Room codes and report reasons need free-text entry with no VR path. `PlayerUI` also reads `Gamepad.current` directly, so a virtual gamepad can press Kick. | A world-space keyboard; do not create a virtual gamepad. |

Next in line: IT-24 binoculars (camera pose and FOV replaced), IT-34 and IT-45 (cannon launch and warp-on-throw with no fade), IC-53 (bad mushroom launch, ragdoll and blind), MF-19 and MF-35 (scene swaps with no or overlay-only loading screens), ST-09 and ST-15 (continuous cold and heat pulses), EN-58 (mirage UVs in screen space).

## (c) Open questions needing in-game observation

Cross-cutting:

- Dump the item prefab data at runtime (`ItemDatabase`): `Hand_R` and `Hand_L` presence, `rightHandOnly`, `defaultPos`, `usingTimePrimary` and the `ItemAction` trigger flags. This fills every Hands cell with measured data and settles which vanilla animations exist.
- Check per-eye rendering of `CameraQuad`, the `Transition` variants, the `ScreenVFX` quads and the Mirage and Blind meshes once stereo works.
- Measure the real prefab values behind hold times and interaction distances (`Interaction.distance`, `area`, `maxCharacterInteractAngle`, `timeToOpen`, `interactTime`, `timeToLight`).

Per area, as recorded while building the rows:

#### Item core and consumables (IC)

- Item families: exact prefab names (bandage, medkit, antidote, specific berries, hot dog, marshmallow, energy items) are in prefabs, not code; list with `sandbox` spawn and `ItemDatabase.itemLookup` at runtime
- Hand class per item: `rightHandOnly`, `Hand_R/Hand_L` transforms and `defaultPos` are prefab data; dump from `ItemDatabase` at runtime to fill the Hands column
- IC-13: which prefabs carry `ItemVFX` subclasses (CursedSkullVFX, WarpCompassVFX) and `useAnimation` string values
- IC-06: whether the vanilla Eat/Drink/Heal pose moves the item to the face by animator only (R1 §5.1 says UNVERIFIED)
- IC-23: whether the player can open their own worn backpack (own back collider excluded from the raycast)
- IC-16: whether `OnPrimaryReleased` / `OnReleased` is used by any prefab-set UnityEvent
- IC-14: effect of the `usingTimePrimary > 0` check inside `ContinueUseSecondary` on secondary-only items
- IC-56: whether the cooked amount is carried onto the leftover item (RPC takes it but the master code ignores it)
- IC-29: which scene props are `FakeItem` and `FakeItemSticky`

#### Tools and special items (IT)

- ItemAction trigger flags (primary vs secondary vs scroll) for flare, parasol, dart gun, warp items, ritual dagger, camera override: prefab data (IT-18, IT-17, IT-35, IT-46, IT-54, IT-25)
- Hand_R/Hand_L grip child presence and rightHandOnly per item: prefab data, so every Hands cell is a design judgement
- Pairing of Action_Antizooka with Action_RaycastSpawnSomething and AntiSphere prefab (IT-36)
- Whether Action_Spawn, Action_LaunchPlayer, Action_OverrideCamera are referenced by any shipped prefab (IT-37, IT-39, IT-25)
- Reachability of the Bing Bong godcam powers: isGodCam is never assigned in code (IT-58)
- Which scene holds the Mirror prop and its render result per eye (IT-30)
- Stuck-item and explosion screen effects from GhostBall and Dynamite (IT-40, IT-55)

#### Interactables (IN)

- IN-01: Interaction.distance/area/maxCharacterInteractAngle are serialized fields, prefab values may differ from defaults 2/0.5/90
- IN-02, IN-26, IN-29, IN-30, IN-32, IN-40: GetInteractTime sources (timeToOpen, interactTime, timeToLight, cookTime) are prefab data, measure actual hold times
- IN-03: UI_Interaction.cs:4 usage in scenes; whether any other component reads Interaction.currentHovered at runtime
- IN-12: which prefabs use TriggerOnInteract and TriggerEvent target
- IN-15: which Item prefabs have canUseOnFriend and secondaryInteractPrompt
- IN-06: whether thorns on own body carry colliders for the ray or only ArrowOnMe/sticky selection is reachable
- IN-13: carried player camera (ghost spectate) and whether carrier sees body in first person; carryPosRef pose from prefab
- IN-22, IN-29, IN-33, IN-34, IN-36: spawn id / location for sandbox spawn of these props
- IN-17: how Helping Hand run option is toggled in the custom-run menu
- IN-20: whether PassOut fade during VR needs replacement (Transitions.instance FadeToBlack is a screen overlay)

#### Character actions (CH)

- CH-50: which animator states attach DisableIKBehavior
- CH-52: PointPinger.coolDown value and ping marker size in stereo
- CH-17: underwater effect and WaterZone defaults
- CH-47: nametag canvas mode
- CH-49: emote asset list (EmoteWheelData)

#### Statuses (ST)

- ST-40: where the IllegalScreenEffect mesh sits (under the camera or a CameraQuad) and how its shader renders per eye; prefab-only
- ST-33, ST-34, ST-32, ST-42: which items apply Glowing, Chaos, Exhaustion, ClimbingChalk (serialized Affliction in Action_ApplyAffliction prefab data; decompile shows no code creators)
- ST-62: LavaPost MeshRenderer shape and what it draws; LavaAlpha global is set only to 0 in Start
- ST-59: stereo behaviour of each VFX_Screen_* quad and exact per-prefab ScreenVFX delay and duration values
- ST-38: whirlwind object visibility from the first-person viewpoint (prefab child of the Character)
- ST-35: whether HotSun bounds, rate and amount use the class defaults in the Mesa prefab
- ST-03: placement of UI_Stamina fill (prefab)
- ST-54: which Transition subtype (CanvasGroup vs Shader mesh) FadeToBlack, PassOut, FallDeath and SpectateSwitch use in the scene
- ST-25 and ST-42: no reader of PreventPoisonHealing or ClimbingChalk exists in Assembly-CSharp; confirm they are inert in game

#### Mobs and hazards (EN)

- EN-16: how the boar bot damages players (prefab CollisionModifier or AOE not in code)
- EN-43: what damages players when a ShakyRock falls (no damage code in class)
- EN-55: component list of geyser, urchin, flash plant, exploding mushroom prefabs
- EN-05: render path of Scoutmaster `mat` overlay (post-process quad or camera effect)
- EN-25: content of the eruption prefab
- EN-07: source of spider Bonk event
- RunSettings Hazard_* gates: only Antlion, FrogTongues, Thorns, PetrifyingStones, Ravens, SleepyGloom, TheGloomRises, TheLavaRises, Tornado, Zombies, BigGhost, Scoutmaster, NapberryHypno and Heat are read by name in code (grep SETTINGTYPE.Hazard_). Bees, Condors, Flytraps, Jellyfish, Spiders, Beetles, Scorpions and the traps gate through prefab or spawn data, so custom-run toggles for them cannot be checked from the decompile
- EN-24: whether LavaPost mesh is a camera-attached quad

#### Meta flows (MF)

- MF-01: whether XR can start before GameBooter runs at BeforeSplashScreen and what the user sees on the first frames
- MF-11: whether MainMenuJoinRoomPage takes the input-field path or the OpenDialog path under SteamVR/Proton, and what OpenDialog shows for text
- MF-17 and swept AchievementManager: whether the Steam overlay (invite dialog, achievement toast) appears inside the headset
- MF-13, MF-22, MF-34: parent canvases and scenes of NextLevelUI, TodaysBiomes, DevMessageUI, PlayerConnectionLog and AscentUI are prefab data
- MF-32: what SteamScreenshots.TriggerScreenshot captures in VR (mirror window vs eye texture) and the strength of the booth flash image
- MF-50, MF-55: which animation events call ContinueFinalCutscene and set Player.doneWithCutscene
- MF-52: EndScreen starts its routine from Start(); confirm the object is inactive in the scene so Start fires on first Open
- MF-80: DynamicResolution has no code references; check whether any scene attaches it
- MF-102: which Transition variant (canvas group or shader mesh) each prefab uses and whether the PassOut/FallDeath prefabs are active
- R2 correction: R2 §1.6 attributes MenuWindow.CloseAllWindows and the win/loss branch to Character.EndGame (L864); that method only raises the RPC and the logic is in RPCEndGame (Character.cs:893). R2 also omits the credits wait (PeakHandler.cs:223), the unused PassOut/FallDeath transitions, PlayerConnectionLog, and that CrossPlatformSetting and AudioModeSetting are unregistered
